    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient experiencing Schizoaffective Disorder (Bipolar Type), in a diagnostic interview with a\npsychiatrist. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\n(this is the start of the consultation)\n\n[Last Question]\nHi, it's good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / automotive_service_technician_or_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"What brought me in is I keep hearing a man’s voice coming from the garage vents and sometimes from under the hood, clear as somebody standing next to me, telling me not to trust my husband or touch certain cars.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… yeah, hi. I’m here because I keep hearing this man’s voice, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just ignore it or tell myself it’s nothing.

It happens at work a lot — I’m a mechanic, so I’m in the garage all day — and I’ll hear it coming through the vents, like the air vents in the shop, but not muffled like someone far away. It’s clear, like there’s a man standing right next to me talking into my ear. And sometimes it’s when I’m under the hood of a car, like the voice is coming from inside the engine bay or underneath something, even when nobody is there.

He says things like I shouldn’t trust my husband, or he’ll tell me not to touch certain cars. And I know that sounds… I know how it sounds. But in the moment it feels so real, like I can hear the direction it’s coming from and everything. It’s been scaring me, and it’s messing with me at work because I’ll hesitate before doing something I’ve done a thousand times. That’s really what brought me in.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… yes. I know how that sounds, I really do. If someone said it to me a few years ago, I’d probably think it was far-fetched too. But it doesn’t feel like just a random voice or just me mishearing something.

It feels intentional. Like there’s a setup around it. Not just the vents, but the stuff in the shop — the EV chargers especially, and the security cameras. I’m pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using them somehow to track what I’m doing. Like when I’m near certain bays, or when I’m working on certain cars, it’s like they already know where I am and what I’m touching.

And they’ll show me footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or they’ll act like I’m making connections that don’t exist, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. Because footage can miss things, or they can choose what to show me, or the tracking isn’t something you’d just see on a regular camera anyway. So… yeah, I think someone is doing it on purpose. I don’t really know exactly how every part works, but I don’t think it’s nothing.

just listened to me I could turn our little shop into the top EV place in the state.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: For weeks at a time I’d sleep maybe two or three hours, wake up before dawn with my mind already in the garage, and feel like sleeping more would just waste time.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: Once I start explaining my plans or what’s been happening, the words pour out so fast my husband and coworkers tell me to slow down, but stopping feels almost impossible.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the times I was barely sleeping and racing around the shop with big plans, I was also hearing the voice from the vents and feeling certain the chargers and cameras were being used to watch me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Most of this past year has been taken over by those high-energy stretches where I talk too fast, take on too much, and feel driven like I can’t slow down.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: Even during a quieter few weeks when I was sleeping more and wasn’t making huge plans, I still heard the garage voice and believed my husband and boss were tracking me.\n- Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The long stretches of little sleep, racing plans, and nonstop activity have been happening for around eleven months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / automotive_service_technician_or_mechanic / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Did this start while you were using alcohol or drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’m not sitting here thinking, like, “Oh yeah, it was because of something I took,” if that’s what you mean. Yeah… I think so. I don’t like saying it because it makes me sound like I’m just being rude or lazy, but there are stretches where I just don’t want to be around anybody.

Like, I’ll see people calling or texting and I just… don’t call them back. Not because I hate them or anything, I just can’t make myself pick up the phone and do the whole conversation. And church stuff used to be a big thing for me — I used to help with the potlucks, like actually be involved, set things up, make sure people had what they needed. Lately I’ll just skip it. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.

And at work it happens too, kind of. I’ll be in the bay and instead of getting moving like I normally would, I’ll just be standing there looking at the tools or the bench, and it’s like I know I should care, I know I should start the next thing, but there’s this blank feeling. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.

So, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. It’s not every single second, but there are definitely stretches where I just stay in the house or keep to myself and everything feels like too much effort.

jobs, plan a car club event, reorganize the parts shelves, and still feel like I need to keep moving.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I was sure I was the only one who really understood where the repair business was headed, like if everybody just listened to me I could turn our little shop into the top EV place in the state.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: Once I start explaining my plans or what’s been happening, the words pour out so fast my husband and coworkers tell me to slow down, but stopping feels almost impossible.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the times I was barely sleeping and racing around the shop with big plans, I was also hearing the voice from the vents and feeling certain the chargers and cameras were being used to watch me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Most of this past year has been taken over by those high-energy stretches where I talk too fast, take on too much, and feel driven like I can’t slow down.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: Even during a quieter few weeks when I was sleeping more and wasn’t making huge plans, I still heard the garage voice and believed my husband and boss were tracking me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve made mistakes at the shop and was pulled off high-voltage EV work because people were worried Symptoms Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: The voices and tracking beliefs have been happening for about ten months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The long stretches of little sleep, racing plans, and nonstop activity have been happening for around eleven months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / automotive_service_technician_or_mechanic / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is actually using the vents or the cars to speak to you on purpose?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes. I know how that sounds, I really do. If someone said it to me a few years ago, I’d probably think it was far-fetched too. But it doesn’t feel like just a random voice or just me mishearing something.\n\nIt feels intentional. Like there’s a setup around it. Not just the vents, but the stuff in the shop — the EV chargers especially, and the security cameras. Like when I’m near certain bays, or when I’m working on certain cars, it’s like they already know where I am and what I’m touching.\n\nAnd they’ll show me footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or they’ll act like I’m making connections that don’t exist, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. I don’t really know exactly how every part works, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this voice?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months now, I think. Not like I can point to the exact day and circle it on a calendar, but around that. Close to a year, but not quite.\n\nAt first I kept thinking maybe it was just something in the shop, like a sound carrying weird or somebody talking where I couldn’t see them, so I didn’t really mark it as “this is the start.” But looking back, it’s been going on for about ten months — the voice, and then the feeling that there’s tracking tied into it too. It hasn’t just been a couple weeks or anything. It’s been long enough that I’ve had time to keep noticing the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past ten months, have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t like saying it because it makes me sound like I’m just being rude or lazy, but there are stretches where I just don’t want to be around anybody.\n\nLike, I’ll see people calling or texting and I just… don’t call them back. Not because I hate them or anything, I just can’t make myself pick up the phone and do the whole conversation. And church stuff used to be a big thing for me — I used to help with the potlucks, like actually be involved, set things up, make sure people had what they needed. Lately I’ll just skip it. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. I’ll be in the bay and instead of getting moving like I normally would, I’ll just be standing there looking at the tools or the bench, and it’s like I know I should care, I know I should start the next thing, but there’s this blank feeling. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. I’m here because I keep hearing this man’s voice, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just ignore it or tell myself it’s nothing.\n\nIt happens at work a lot — I’m a mechanic, so I’m in the garage all day — and I’ll hear it coming through the vents, like the air vents in the shop, but not muffled like someone far away. It’s clear, like there’s a man standing right next to me talking into my ear. And sometimes it’s when I’m under the hood of a car, like the voice is coming from inside the engine bay or underneath something, even when nobody is there.\n\nHe says things like I shouldn’t trust my husband, or he’ll tell me not to touch certain cars. And I know that sounds… I know how it sounds. But in the moment it feels so real, like I can hear the direction it’s coming from and everything. It’s been scaring me, and it’s messing with me at work because I’ll hesitate before doing something I’ve done a thousand times. That’s really what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Did this start while you were using alcohol or drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’m not sitting here thinking, like, “Oh yeah, it was because of something I took,” if that’s what you mean. I haven’t connected it to that in my head.\n\nI guess I’d have to think about it more, because the way it started didn’t feel like some one-night thing where I used something and then heard something right after. It felt more like it started creeping into regular life, if that makes sense. Like at work, during a normal day, doing normal stuff. So… I’m not sure. It’s been long enough that I’ve had time to keep noticing the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past ten months, have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. It’s not every single second, but there are definitely stretches where I just stay in the house or keep to myself and everything feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine or cocaine during the time you’ve been hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how you want me to answer that. It’s clear, like there’s a man standing right next to me talking into my ear. Not just the vents, but the stuff in the shop — the EV chargers especially, and the security cameras. I’m pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using them somehow to track what I’m doing. Like when I’m near certain bays, or when I’m working on certain cars, it’s like they already know where I am and what I’m touching.\n\nAnd they’ll show me footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or they’ll act like I’m making connections that don’t exist, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. Because footage can miss things, or they can choose what to show me, or the tracking isn’t something you’d just see on a regular camera anyway. So… yeah, I think someone is doing it on purpose. I don’t really know exactly how every part works, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this voice?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months now, I think. Close to a year, but not quite.\n\nAt first I kept thinking maybe it was just something in the shop, like a sound carrying weird or somebody talking where I couldn’t see them, so I didn’t really mark it as “this is the start.” But looking back, it’s been going on for about ten months — the voice, and then the feeling that there’s tracking tied into it too. It hasn’t just been a couple weeks or anything. I don’t like saying it because it makes me sound like I’m just being rude or lazy, but there are stretches where I just don’t want to be around anybody.\n\nLike, I’ll see people calling or texting and I just… don’t call them back. Not because I hate them or anything, I just can’t make myself pick up the phone and do the whole conversation. And church stuff used to be a big thing for me — I used to help with the potlucks, like actually be involved, set things up, make sure people had what they needed. Lately I’ll just skip it. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. I’ll be in the bay and instead of getting moving like I normally would, I’ll just be standing there looking at the tools or the bench, and it’s like I know I should care, I know I should start the next thing, but there’s this blank feeling. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern like that.\n\nI mean, I understand what you’re asking — like whether after I stop drinking, then the voice or that tracked feeling kicks up — but I don’t have it mapped out in my head that neatly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yes, that did happen. Not just like one bad night of sleep, either. There were stretches where for weeks at a time I’d only sleep maybe two or three hours, and then I’d wake up before dawn already wide awake, like my head was already at the garage before my body was even up.

It didn’t feel like normal insomnia, where you’re exhausted and miserable and just can’t sleep. It was more like… sleeping felt like a waste of time. Like if I stayed in bed, I was losing hours I could be using. I’d be thinking about work, tools, cars, parts, things I wanted to fix or organize, and my mind would already be running before the sun was up.

And at the time I wouldn’t always think, “This is unusual.” I’d kind of tell myself I was just being productive or that I had momentum. But looking back, yeah, it was definitely much less sleep than usual, and I still felt like I had energy to keep going.

I don’t like saying it because it makes me sound like I’m just being rude or lazy, but there are stretches where I just don’t want to be around anybody.\n\nLike, I’ll see people calling or texting and I just… don’t call them back. Not because I hate them or anything, I just can’t make myself pick up the phone and do the whole conversation. And church stuff used to be a big thing for me — I used to help with the potlucks, like actually be involved, set things up, make sure people had what they needed. Lately I’ll just skip it. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. I’ll be in the bay and instead of getting moving like I normally would, I’ll just be standing there looking at the tools or the bench, and it’s like I know I should care, I know I should start the next thing, but there’s this blank feeling. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. It’s not every single second, but there are definitely stretches where I just stay in the house or keep to myself and everything feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine or cocaine during the time you’ve been hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how you want me to answer that. Like, do you mean any amount at all, even once, or do you mean regularly using it around the time the voice started?\n\nI get why you’re asking, because people always jump to “it must be drugs” when someone says they’re hearing something, but I don’t really have a neat answer ready in my head for that. If you mean something specific by “used,” maybe you can tell me exactly what counts, and I can try to answer it more clearly.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you used methamphetamine?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer that cleanly. I hear you asking specifically methamphetamine, and I’m not trying to dodge it, I just… I’m not sure what counts in the way you’re asking.\n\nLike, I don’t have a clear “yes, this was happening every week” kind of answer sitting in my head. If you mean, was I regularly using meth during this whole ten months, I don’t think that’s how I’d describe it. But if you mean any exposure, any time, even if it was around people or something happened once, then I’d have to think harder because my memory around all of it isn’t organized neatly.\n\nI know that probably sounds evasive. I’m sorry. I just don’t want to say yes or no and then have it be the wrong kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling of being tracked ever started or gotten worse after you stopped drinking alcohol?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern like that.\n\nI mean, I understand what you’re asking — like whether after I stop drinking, then the voice or that tracked feeling kicks up — but I don’t have it mapped out in my head that neatly. When things get bad, I’m usually already stressed and not sleeping right or just trying to get through the day, so I don’t always stop and think, “Okay, did this happen after alcohol or before alcohol?”\n\nSo… I can’t honestly say yes to that. I don’t remember sitting there thinking, “Oh, I stopped drinking and now this is worse.” It doesn’t stand out to me that way. If you need me to, I can try to think through specific times, but just answering right now, I don’t know that I’ve noticed that connection.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you had a period when you felt unusually energetic and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. Not just like one bad night of sleep, either. There were stretches where for weeks at a time I’d only sleep maybe two or three hours, and then I’d wake up before dawn already wide awake, like my head was already at the garage before my body was even up.\n\nIt didn’t feel like normal insomnia, where you’re exhausted and miserable and just can’t sleep. It was more like… sleeping felt like a waste of time. Like if I stayed in bed, I was losing hours I could be using. I’d be thinking about work, tools, cars, parts, things I wanted to fix or organize, and my mind would already be running before the sun was up.\n\nAnd at the time I wouldn’t always think, “This is unusual.” I’d kind of tell myself I was just being productive or that I had momentum. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. Yeah, that was happening then too.

When I was in those stretches where I was barely sleeping — like two, three hours and then right back up, already thinking about the shop and what I was going to do — that’s when the voice was there a lot. I’d be at work, moving fast, making plans in my head, thinking I could reorganize things or get ahead on everything, and I’d hear it through the vents. It felt like it was coming from the vent system, not like just a thought.

And during that same time I was also really sure the chargers and cameras were being used to watch me. Like I’d look at them and it felt connected, like they weren’t just regular equipment sitting there. So yes, when I was sleeping that little and running around with all these big plans, the voice and the feeling of being watched were both going on.

I mean, I don’t think I black out or anything like that, if that’s what you mean. I’m usually still aware I’m at work, I know what I’m doing, I can see the car in front of me and hear the normal shop noises too.\n\nBut it does kind of… pull me out of the moment, I guess. Like my attention snaps to the voice because it feels so close and specific, and then I’m standing there trying to figure out if someone actually said something or if it came from the vent or from under the hood. So I can get distracted and rattled, and maybe I look confused from the outside, but I don’t think I’m totally unaware of what’s going on around me.\n\nIt’s more like everything else is still there, but the voice becomes the thing I’m focused on.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is actually using the vents or the cars to speak to you on purpose?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes. I know how that sounds, I really do. If someone said it to me a few years ago, I’d probably think it was far-fetched too. But it doesn’t feel like just a random voice or just me mishearing something.\n\nIt feels intentional. Like there’s a setup around it. Not just the vents, but the stuff in the shop — the EV chargers especially, and the security cameras. I’m pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using them somehow to track what I’m doing. Like when I’m near certain bays, or when I’m working on certain cars, it’s like they already know where I am and what I’m touching.\n\nAnd they’ll show me footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or they’ll act like I’m making connections that don’t exist, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. Because footage can miss things, or they can choose what to show me, or the tracking isn’t something you’d just see on a regular camera anyway. So… yeah, I think someone is doing it on purpose. I don’t really know exactly how every part works, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this voice?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months now, I think. Not like I can point to the exact day and circle it on a calendar, but around that. Close to a year, but not quite.\n\nAt first I kept thinking maybe it was just something in the shop, like a sound carrying weird or somebody talking where I couldn’t see them, so I didn’t really mark it as “this is the start.” But looking back, it’s been going on for about ten months — the voice, and then the feeling that there’s tracking tied into it too. It hasn’t just been a couple weeks or anything. It’s been long enough that I’ve had time to keep noticing the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past ten months, have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t like saying it because it makes me sound like I’m just being rude or lazy, but there are stretches where I just don’t want to be around anybody.\n\nLike, I’ll see people calling or texting and I just… don’t call them back. Not because I hate them or anything, I just can’t make myself pick up the phone and do the whole conversation. And church stuff used to be a big thing for me — I used to help with the potlucks, like actually be involved, set things up, make sure people had what they needed. Lately I’ll just skip it. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. I’ll be in the bay and instead of getting moving like I normally would, I’ll just be standing there looking at the tools or the bench, and it’s like I know I should care, I know I should start the next thing, but there’s this blank feeling. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. It’s not every single second, but there are definitely stretches where I just stay in the house or keep to myself and everything feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine or cocaine during the time you’ve been hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how you want me to answer that. Like, do you mean any amount at all, even once, or do you mean regularly using it around the time the voice started?\n\nI get why you’re asking, because people always jump to “it must be drugs” when someone says they’re hearing something, but I don’t really have a neat answer ready in my head for that. If you mean something specific by “used,” maybe you can tell me exactly what counts, and I can try to answer it more clearly.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you used methamphetamine?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer that cleanly. I hear you asking specifically methamphetamine, and I’m not trying to dodge it, I just… I’m not sure what counts in the way you’re asking.\n\nLike, I don’t have a clear “yes, this was happening every week” kind of answer sitting in my head. If you mean, was I regularly using meth during this whole ten months, I don’t think that’s how I’d describe it. I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern like that.\n\nI mean, I understand what you’re asking — like whether after I stop drinking, then the voice or that tracked feeling kicks up — but I don’t have it mapped out in my head that neatly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… there were times like that too.

It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because when things are really intense everything kind of overlaps, but yes — there were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t in that sped-up mode. I was sleeping more, not just two or three hours, and I wasn’t waking up with all these huge plans like I had to fix everything or reorganize the whole garage or whatever.

My mood felt more… regular, I guess. Not amazing, not terrible, just more like myself. But the voice didn’t completely go away. I would still hear it at the garage, like coming from that same area, the vents or the building somehow. And even though I wasn’t feeling all revved up, I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing.

That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, because I can’t just say it only happened when my mood was really up. Even when I was more calm and sleeping more normally, it was still there.

I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. Like when I’m near certain bays, or when I’m working on certain cars, it’s like they already know where I am and what I’m touching.\n\nAnd they’ll show me footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or they’ll act like I’m making connections that don’t exist, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. Because footage can miss things, or they can choose what to show me, or the tracking isn’t something you’d just see on a regular camera anyway. So… yeah, I think someone is doing it on purpose. I don’t really know exactly how every part works, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this voice?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months now, I think. Not like I can point to the exact day and circle it on a calendar, but around that. Close to a year, but not quite.\n\nAt first I kept thinking maybe it was just something in the shop, like a sound carrying weird or somebody talking where I couldn’t see them, so I didn’t really mark it as “this is the start.” But looking back, it’s been going on for about ten months — the voice, and then the feeling that there’s tracking tied into it too. It hasn’t just been a couple weeks or anything. It’s been long enough that I’ve had time to keep noticing the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past ten months, have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t like saying it because it makes me sound like I’m just being rude or lazy, but there are stretches where I just don’t want to be around anybody.\n\nLike, I’ll see people calling or texting and I just… don’t call them back. Not because I hate them or anything, I just can’t make myself pick up the phone and do the whole conversation. And church stuff used to be a big thing for me — I used to help with the potlucks, like actually be involved, set things up, make sure people had what they needed. Lately I’ll just skip it. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. I’ll be in the bay and instead of getting moving like I normally would, I’ll just be standing there looking at the tools or the bench, and it’s like I know I should care, I know I should start the next thing, but there’s this blank feeling. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. It’s not every single second, but there are definitely stretches where I just stay in the house or keep to myself and everything feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine or cocaine during the time you’ve been hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how you want me to answer that. Like, do you mean any amount at all, even once, or do you mean regularly using it around the time the voice started?\n\nI get why you’re asking, because people always jump to “it must be drugs” when someone says they’re hearing something, but I don’t really have a neat answer ready in my head for that. If you mean something specific by “used,” maybe you can tell me exactly what counts, and I can try to answer it more clearly.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you used methamphetamine?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer that cleanly. I hear you asking specifically methamphetamine, and I’m not trying to dodge it, I just… I’m not sure what counts in the way you’re asking.\n\nLike, I don’t have a clear “yes, this was happening every week” kind of answer sitting in my head. If you mean, was I regularly using meth during this whole ten months, I don’t think that’s how I’d describe it. But if you mean any exposure, any time, even if it was around people or something happened once, then I’d have to think harder because my memory around all of it isn’t organized neatly.\n\nI know that probably sounds evasive. I’m sorry. I just don’t want to say yes or no and then have it be the wrong kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling of being tracked ever started or gotten worse after you stopped drinking alcohol?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern like that.\n\nI mean, I understand what you’re asking — like whether after I stop drinking, then the voice or that tracked feeling kicks up — but I don’t have it mapped out in my head that neatly. When things get bad, I’m usually already stressed and not sleeping right or just trying to get through the day, so I don’t always stop and think, “Okay, did this happen after alcohol or before alcohol?”\n\nSo… I can’t honestly say yes to that. I don’t remember sitting there thinking, “Oh, I stopped drinking and now this is worse.” It doesn’t stand out to me that way. If you need me to, I can try to think through specific times, but just answering right now, I don’t know that I’ve noticed that connection.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you had a period when you felt unusually energetic and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. Not just like one bad night of sleep, either. There were stretches where for weeks at a time I’d only sleep maybe two or three hours, and then I’d wake up before dawn already wide awake, like my head was already at the garage before my body was even up.\n\nIt didn’t feel like normal insomnia, where you’re exhausted and miserable and just can’t sleep. It was more like… sleeping felt like a waste of time. Like if I stayed in bed, I was losing hours I could be using. I’d be thinking about work, tools, cars, parts, things I wanted to fix or organize, and my mind would already be running before the sun was up.\n\nAnd at the time I wouldn’t always think, “This is unusual.” I’d kind of tell myself I was just being productive or that I had momentum. But looking back, yeah, it was definitely much less sleep than usual, and I still felt like I had energy to keep going.\n\nDoctor: When you were sleeping only two or three hours a night, were you hearing the voice then?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yeah, that was happening then too.\n\nWhen I was in those stretches where I was barely sleeping — like two, three hours and then right back up, already thinking about the shop and what I was going to do — that’s when the voice was there a lot. I’d be at work, moving fast, making plans in my head, thinking I could reorganize things or get ahead on everything, and I’d hear it through the vents. It felt like it was coming from the vent system, not like just a thought.\n\nAnd during that same time I was also really sure the chargers and cameras were being used to watch me. Like I’d look at them and it felt connected, like they weren’t just regular equipment sitting there. So yes, when I was sleeping that little and running around with all these big plans, the voice and the feeling of being watched were both going on.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when you were hearing the voice even though your mood felt normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… there were times like that too.\n\nIt’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because when things are really intense everything kind of overlaps, but yes — there were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t in that sped-up mode. I was sleeping more, not just two or three hours, and I wasn’t waking up with all these huge plans like I had to fix everything or reorganize the whole garage or whatever.\n\nMy mood felt more… regular, I guess. Not amazing, not terrible, just more like myself. But the voice didn’t completely go away. I would still hear it at the garage, like coming from that same area, the vents or the building somehow. And even though I wasn’t feeling all revved up, I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing.\n\nThat’s part of what makes it hard to explain, because I can’t just say it only happened when my mood was really up. Even when I was more calm and sleeping more normally, it was still there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, hi. I’m here because I keep hearing this man’s voice, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just ignore it or tell myself it’s nothing.\n\nIt happens at work a lot — I’m a mechanic, so I’m in the garage all day — and I’ll hear it coming through the vents, like the air vents in the shop, but not muffled like someone far away. It’s clear, like there’s a man standing right next to me talking into my ear. And sometimes it’s when I’m under the hood of a car, like the voice is coming from inside the engine bay or underneath something, even when nobody is there.\n\nHe says things like I shouldn’t trust my husband, or he’ll tell me not to touch certain cars. I haven’t connected it to that in my head.\n\nI guess I’d have to think about it more, because the way it started didn’t feel like some one-night thing where I used something and then heard something right after. It felt more like it started creeping into regular life, if that makes sense. Like at work, during a normal day, doing normal stuff. So… I’m not sure. I don’t think I can point to alcohol or drugs as the start of it.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voice, do you also feel confused or not fully aware of what’s going on around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think I black out or anything like that, if that’s what you mean. I’m usually still aware I’m at work, I know what I’m doing, I can see the car in front of me and hear the normal shop noises too.\n\nBut it does kind of… pull me out of the moment, I guess. Like my attention snaps to the voice because it feels so close and specific, and then I’m standing there trying to figure out if someone actually said something or if it came from the vent or from under the hood. So I can get distracted and rattled, and maybe I look confused from the outside, but I don’t think I’m totally unaware of what’s going on around me.\n\nIt’s more like everything else is still there, but the voice becomes the thing I’m focused on.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is actually using the vents or the cars to speak to you on purpose?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes. I know how that sounds, I really do. If someone said it to me a few years ago, I’d probably think it was far-fetched too. But it doesn’t feel like just a random voice or just me mishearing something.\n\nIt feels intentional. Like there’s a setup around it. Not just the vents, but the stuff in the shop — the EV chargers especially, and the security cameras. I’m pretty convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using them somehow to track what I’m doing. Like when I’m near certain bays, or when I’m working on certain cars, it’s like they already know where I am and what I’m touching.\n\nAnd they’ll show me footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or they’ll act like I’m making connections that don’t exist, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. Because footage can miss things, or they can choose what to show me, or the tracking isn’t something you’d just see on a regular camera anyway. So… yeah, I think someone is doing it on purpose. I don’t really know exactly how every part works, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this voice?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months now, I think. Not like I can point to the exact day and circle it on a calendar, but around that. Close to a year, but not quite.\n\nAt first I kept thinking maybe it was just something in the shop, like a sound carrying weird or somebody talking where I couldn’t see them, so I didn’t really mark it as “this is the start.” But looking back, it’s been going on for about ten months — the voice, and then the feeling that there’s tracking tied into it too. It hasn’t just been a couple weeks or anything. It’s been long enough that I’ve had time to keep noticing the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past ten months, have you become much less interested in being around other people than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t like saying it because it makes me sound like I’m just being rude or lazy, but there are stretches where I just don’t want to be around anybody.\n\nLike, I’ll see people calling or texting and I just… don’t call them back. Not because I hate them or anything, I just can’t make myself pick up the phone and do the whole conversation. And church stuff used to be a big thing for me — I used to help with the potlucks, like actually be involved, set things up, make sure people had what they needed. Lately I’ll just skip it. I’ll tell myself I’m tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t go.\n\nAnd at work it happens too, kind of. I’ll be in the bay and instead of getting moving like I normally would, I’ll just be standing there looking at the tools or the bench, and it’s like I know I should care, I know I should start the next thing, but there’s this blank feeling. Like I can’t get interested in any of it.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve pulled back from people. More than I used to. It’s not every single second, but there are definitely stretches where I just stay in the house or keep to myself and everything feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you used methamphetamine or cocaine during the time you’ve been hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how you want me to answer that. Like, do you mean any amount at all, even once, or do you mean regularly using it around the time the voice started?\n\nI get why you’re asking, because people always jump to “it must be drugs” when someone says they’re hearing something, but I don’t really have a neat answer ready in my head for that. If you mean something specific by “used,” maybe you can tell me exactly what counts, and I can try to answer it more clearly.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you used methamphetamine?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how to answer that cleanly. I hear you asking specifically methamphetamine, and I’m not trying to dodge it, I just… I’m not sure what counts in the way you’re asking.\n\nLike, I don’t have a clear “yes, this was happening every week” kind of answer sitting in my head. If you mean, was I regularly using meth during this whole ten months, I don’t think that’s how I’d describe it. But if you mean any exposure, any time, even if it was around people or something happened once, then I’d have to think harder because my memory around all of it isn’t organized neatly.\n\nI know that probably sounds evasive. I’m sorry. I just don’t want to say yes or no and then have it be the wrong kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling of being tracked ever started or gotten worse after you stopped drinking alcohol?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed a clear pattern like that.\n\nI mean, I understand what you’re asking — like whether after I stop drinking, then the voice or that tracked feeling kicks up — but I don’t have it mapped out in my head that neatly. When things get bad, I’m usually already stressed and not sleeping right or just trying to get through the day, so I don’t always stop and think, “Okay, did this happen after alcohol or before alcohol?”\n\nSo… I can’t honestly say yes to that. I don’t remember sitting there thinking, “Oh, I stopped drinking and now this is worse.” It doesn’t stand out to me that way. If you need me to, I can try to think through specific times, but just answering right now, I don’t know that I’ve noticed that connection.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you had a period when you felt unusually energetic and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. Not just like one bad night of sleep, either. There were stretches where for weeks at a time I’d only sleep maybe two or three hours, and then I’d wake up before dawn already wide awake, like my head was already at the garage before my body was even up.\n\nIt didn’t feel like normal insomnia, where you’re exhausted and miserable and just can’t sleep. It was more like… sleeping felt like a waste of time. Like if I stayed in bed, I was losing hours I could be using. I’d be thinking about work, tools, cars, parts, things I wanted to fix or organize, and my mind would already be running before the sun was up.\n\nAnd at the time I wouldn’t always think, “This is unusual.” I’d kind of tell myself I was just being productive or that I had momentum. But looking back, yeah, it was definitely much less sleep than usual, and I still felt like I had energy to keep going.\n\nDoctor: When you were sleeping only two or three hours a night, were you hearing the voice then?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yeah, that was happening then too.\n\nWhen I was in those stretches where I was barely sleeping — like two, three hours and then right back up, already thinking about the shop and what I was going to do — that’s when the voice was there a lot. I’d be at work, moving fast, making plans in my head, thinking I could reorganize things or get ahead on everything, and I’d hear it through the vents. It felt like it was coming from the vent system, not like just a thought.\n\nAnd during that same time I was also really sure the chargers and cameras were being used to watch me. Like I’d look at them and it felt connected, like they weren’t just regular equipment sitting there. So yes, when I was sleeping that little and running around with all these big plans, the voice and the feeling of being watched were both going on.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when you were hearing the voice even though your mood felt normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… there were times like that too.\n\nIt’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because when things are really intense everything kind of overlaps, but yes — there were a few quieter weeks where I wasn’t in that sped-up mode. I was sleeping more, not just two or three hours, and I wasn’t waking up with all these huge plans like I had to fix everything or reorganize the whole garage or whatever.\n\nMy mood felt more… regular, I guess. Not amazing, not terrible, just more like myself. But the voice didn’t completely go away. I would still hear it at the garage, like coming from that same area, the vents or the building somehow. And even though I wasn’t feeling all revved up, I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing.\n\nThat’s part of what makes it hard to explain, because I can’t just say it only happened when my mood was really up. Even when I was more calm and sleeping more normally, it was still there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean… I’ve had bad days, and I’ve had times where I felt worn down or discouraged, but I don’t know if I’d say there was a solid two weeks where I was depressed most of the day nearly every day.\n\nWhen you say depressed, do you mean like crying and can’t get out of bed, or just feeling low and heavy? Because I can get overwhelmed, but it’s hard for me to separate that from being stressed or exhausted. I don’t want to say yes if it wasn’t really that. I just… I’m not sure I’ve noticed a period like that in the way you’re describing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. Definitely more than a week.

It wasn’t just like one weird weekend or a few nights where I couldn’t sleep. Those stretches with barely any sleep, and me feeling like I had all this energy and all these plans running through my head, they’ve been going on for… I’d say around eleven months now. Not every single second exactly the same, but the pattern has been there for that long.

There were times I’d sleep maybe two or three hours and still get up feeling like I needed to be moving, fixing things, doing more, like I couldn’t slow down. And it would keep going past a week — way past a week. It kind of became normal to me after a while, even though looking back, I guess it wasn’t normal.

When things get bad, I’m usually already stressed and not sleeping right or just trying to get through the day, so I don’t always stop and think, “Okay, did this happen after alcohol or before alcohol?”\n\nSo… I can’t honestly say yes to that. I don’t remember sitting there thinking, “Oh, I stopped drinking and now this is worse.” It doesn’t stand out to me that way. If you need me to, I can try to think through specific times, but just answering right now, I don’t know that I’ve noticed that connection.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you had a period when you felt unusually energetic and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. Not just like one bad night of sleep, either. There were stretches where for weeks at a time I’d only sleep maybe two or three hours, and then I’d wake up before dawn already wide awake, like my head was already at the garage before my body was even up.\n\nIt didn’t feel like normal insomnia, where you’re exhausted and miserable and just can’t sleep. It was more like… sleeping felt like a waste of time. Like if I stayed in bed, I was losing hours I could be using. I’d be thinking about work, tools, cars, parts, things I wanted to fix or organize, and my mind would already be running before the sun was up.\n\nAnd at the time I wouldn’t always think, “This is unusual.” I’d kind of tell myself I was just being productive or that I had momentum. But looking back, yeah, it was definitely much less sleep than usual, and I still felt like I had energy to keep going.\n\nDoctor: When you were sleeping only two or three hours a night, were you hearing the voice then?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yeah, that was happening then too.\n\nWhen I was in those stretches where I was barely sleeping — like two, three hours and then right back up, already thinking about the shop and what I was going to do — that’s when the voice was there a lot. I’d be at work, moving fast, making plans in my head, thinking I could reorganize things or get ahead on everything, and I’d hear it through the vents. It felt like it was coming from the vent system, not like just a thought.\n\nAnd during that same time I was also really sure the chargers and cameras were being used to watch me. Like I’d look at them and it felt connected, like they weren’t just regular equipment sitting there. Yes… I think so. That’s actually what I was trying to explain before, but I know I kind of talk around it.

There were a few weeks where I wasn’t in that really high-energy state. I was sleeping more normally, or at least more than before, and I wasn’t waking up with my head full of giant plans or feeling like I had to do everything right then. I felt quieter, more regular. Not really depressed either, not like I was down all day or anything like that.

But even then, the voice was still there. I’d still hear it around the garage, like it was coming through the building or the vents or somewhere I couldn’t quite pin down. And I still felt like my husband and my boss were tracking me, watching what I was doing somehow. That part didn’t just shut off because my mood calmed down.

So yes, I’d say it did last at least two weeks during a time when my mood wasn’t especially high or especially low.

Like when I’m near certain bays, or when I’m working on certain cars, it’s like they already know where I am and what I’m touching.\n\nAnd they’ll show me footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or they’ll act like I’m making connections that don’t exist, but that doesn’t really settle it for me. Not just like one bad night of sleep, either. There were stretches where for weeks at a time I’d only sleep maybe two or three hours, and then I’d wake up before dawn already wide awake, like my head was already at the garage before my body was even up.\n\nIt didn’t feel like normal insomnia, where you’re exhausted and miserable and just can’t sleep. It was more like… sleeping felt like a waste of time. Like if I stayed in bed, I was losing hours I could be using. I’d be thinking about work, tools, cars, parts, things I wanted to fix or organize, and my mind would already be running before the sun was up.\n\nAnd at the time I wouldn’t always think, “This is unusual.” I’d kind of tell myself I was just being productive or that I had momentum. I was sleeping more, not just two or three hours, and I wasn’t waking up with all these huge plans like I had to fix everything or reorganize the whole garage or whatever.\n\nMy mood felt more… regular, I guess.
